 To determine the profit, we first need to calculate the new value of the house after the repairs. The value increased by 150%, so the new value is the original value plus 150% of the original value. 

Original value of the house: $80,000
Increase in value: 150% of $80,000 = 1.5 * $80,000 = $120,000
New value of the house: $80,000 + $120,000 = $200,000

Next, we add the cost of repairs to the original purchase price to find the total cost:
Total cost = Original price + Repairs = $80,000 + $50,000 = $130,000

Finally, we calculate the profit by subtracting the total cost from the new value of the house:
Profit = New value - Total cost = $200,000 - $130,000 = $70,000

 To solve this problem, we need to calculate the total cost of 16 glasses, considering the discount on every second glass. 

1. **Identify the pattern of pricing**: 
   - The first glass costs $5.
   - The second glass costs 60% of $5, which is $5 * 0.6 = $3.
   - This pattern repeats for every pair of glasses.

2. **Calculate the cost for one pair of glasses**:
   - The cost for one pair (1st and 2nd glass) is $5 + $3 = $8.

3. **Determine the number of pairs in 16 glasses**:
   - Since 16 glasses form 8 pairs, we multiply the cost of one pair by the number of pairs.
   - Total cost = 8 pairs * $8 per pair = $64.

Thus, Kylar needs to pay $64 for 16 glasses.

To find the total number of sheep, we sum the sheep in all three cities: T + C + S = 160 + 80 + 20 = 260.

 Let's break down the problem into steps to find the total time it takes for Carla to download the file.

1. **Initial Download Before Restart:**
   - Carla downloads 2 GB per minute.
   - 40% of 200 GB is $0.40 \times 200 = 80$ GB.
   - Time to download 80 GB at 2 GB/minute is $80 \div  2 = 40$ minutes.

2. **Restart and Download from Beginning:**
   - After the restart, Carla has to start over, so she needs to download the entire 200 GB again.
   - Time to download 200 GB at 2 GB/minute is $200 \div  2 = 100$ minutes.

3. **Total Time:**
   - Time before restart: 40 minutes.
   - Restart time: 20 minutes.
   - Time after restart: 100 minutes.
   - Total time is $40 + 20 + 100 = 160$ minutes.

Therefore, the total time it takes for Carla to download the file is 160 minutes.

   - Time to download 200 GB at 2 GB/minute is $200 \div  2 = 100$ minutes.

3. **Total Time:**
   - Time before restart: 40 minutes.
   - Restart time: 20 minutes.
   - Time after restart: 100 minutes.
   - Total time is $40 + 20 + 100 = 160$ minutes.

Therefore, the total time it takes for Carla to download the file is 160 minutes.

 To solve this problem, we need to calculate the total distance John travels and then determine how far he is from home at the end of the 4 hours.

First, let's calculate the distance John travels in the first 3 hours:
Distance = Speed × Time
Distance = 60 mph × 3 hours = 180 miles

Now, let's calculate the distance John travels in the next 4 hours:
- For the first 2 hours, he is in standstill traffic, so he doesn't cover any distance.
- For the next half-hour, he drives at 30 mph:
Distance = 30 mph × 0.5 hours = 15 miles
- For the remaining 1.5 hours, he drives at 80 mph:
Distance = 80 mph × 1.5 hours = 120 miles

Total distance traveled in the next 4 hours = 15 miles + 120 miles = 135 miles

Since John initially traveled 180 miles away from home and then traveled 135 miles back towards home, we can calculate his distance from home at the end of the 4 hours:
Distance from home = 180 miles - 135 miles = 45 miles

Therefore, John is 45 miles away from home at the end of the 4 hours.

#### 45

- 2 hours in standstill traffic: 0 miles.
- 0.5 hours at 30 mph: $0.5 \times 30 = 15$ miles.
- 1.5 hours at 80 mph: $1.5 \times 80 = 120$ miles.

Total distance back: $15 + 120 = 135$ miles.

Distance from home: $180 - 135 = 45$ miles.

 To solve this problem, we need to calculate the total distance John travels in each segment of his journey and then determine his final position relative to his home.

1. **First Segment (Driving away from home):**
   - John drives for 3 hours at 60 mph.
   - Distance = Speed × Time = 60 mph × 3 hours = 180 miles.

2. **Second Segment (Returning home):**
   - John tries to get home in 4 hours.
   - First 2 hours: Standstill traffic, so distance covered = 0 miles.
   - Next 0.5 hours: Driving at 30 mph.
     - Distance = 30 mph × 0.5 hours = 15 miles.
   - Remaining time = 4 hours - 0.5 hours = 3.5 hours.
   - For the remaining 3.5 hours, he drives at 80 mph.
     - Distance = 80 mph × 3.5 hours = 280 miles.

3. **Total Distance Traveled While Returning:**
   - Total distance = 0 miles (standstill) + 15 miles + 280 miles = 295 miles.

4. **Net Distance from Home:**
   - John initially drove 180 miles away from home.
   - He then traveled 295 miles back towards home.
   - Net distance from home = 180 miles - 295 miles = -115 miles.

Since distance cannot be negative, the absolute value represents the distance from home, which is 115 miles. However, since the question asks how far he is from home at the end of those 4 hours, we consider the net distance, which is 115 miles. But since he was initially 180 miles away and came back 295 miles, he is actually 115 miles closer to home than his starting point, but still 180 - 115 = 65 miles away from home.

 To calculate Eliza's earnings for the week, we need to consider her regular pay for the first 40 hours and her overtime pay for the additional hours.

Regular pay = 40 hours * $10/hour = $400.

Next, we calculate her overtime pay. She worked 45 hours in total, so the overtime hours are 45 - 40 = 5 hours. Her overtime rate is 1.2 times her regular hourly rate, which is 1.2 * $10 = $12 per hour.

 To calculate Eliza's earnings for the week, we need to consider her regular pay for the first 40 hours and her overtime pay for the additional hours. 

First, we calculate her regular pay:
Regular pay = 40 hours * $10/hour = $400.

Next, we calculate her overtime pay. She worked 45 hours in total, so the overtime hours are 45 - 40 = 5 hours. Her overtime rate is 1.2 times her regular hourly rate, which is 1.2 * $10 = $12 per hour.

Overtime pay = 5 hours * $12/hour = $60.

Finally, we add her regular pay and overtime pay to find her total earnings:
Total earnings = Regular pay + Overtime pay

 To calculate Eliza's earnings for the week, we need to consider her regular pay for the first 40 hours and her overtime pay for the additional hours. 

First, we calculate her regular pay:
Regular pay = 40 hours * $10/hour = $400.

Next, we calculate her overtime pay. She worked 45 hours in total, so the overtime hours are 45 - 40 = 5 hours. Her overtime rate is 1.2 times her regular hourly rate, which is 1.2 * $10 = $12 per hour.

Overtime pay = 5 hours * $12/hour = $60.

Finally, we add her regular pay and overtime pay to find her total earnings:
Total earnings = Regular pay + Overtime pay = $400 + $60 = $460.

 To determine the profit, we first need to calculate the new value of the house after the repairs. The value increased by 150%, so the new value is the original value plus 150% of the original value. 

Original value of the house: $80,000
Increase in value: 150% of $80,000 = 1.5 * $80,000 = $120,000

Profit = New value - Total cost = $200,000 - $130,000 = $70,000

 To determine the profit, we first need to calculate the new value of the house after the repairs. The value increased by 150%, so the new value is the original value plus 150% of the original value. 

Original value of the house: $80,000
Increase in value: 150% of $80,000 = 1.5 * $80,000 = $120,000
New value of the house: $80,000 + $120,000 = $200,000

Next, we add the cost of repairs to the original purchase price to find the total cost:
Total cost = Original price + Repairs = $80,000 + $50,000 = $130,000

Finally, we calculate the profit by subtracting the total cost from the new value of the house:
Profit = New value - Total cost = $200,000 - $130,000 = $70,000

Since Wendi feeds each of her chickens three cups of feed per day, and she has a flock of 20 chickens, the total amount of feed needed per day is 3 cups per chicken * 20 chickens = 60 cups of feed. To find the total number of sheep, we sum the sheep in all three cities: T + C + S = 160 + 80 + 20 = 260.

 Let's break down the problem into steps to find the total time it takes for Carla to download the file.

1. **Initial Download Before Restart:**
   - Carla downloads 2 GB per minute.

 To solve this problem, we need to calculate the total distance John travels in each segment of his journey and then determine his final position relative to his home.

1. **First Segment (Driving away from home):**
   - John drives for 3 hours at 60 mph.
   - Distance = Speed × Time = 60 mph × 3 hours = 180 miles.

2. **Second Segment (Returning home):**
   - John tries to get home in 4 hours.
   - First 2 hours: Standstill traffic, so distance covered = 0 miles.
   - Next 0.5 hours: Driving at 30 mph.
     - Distance = 30 mph × 0.5 hours = 15 miles.
   - Remaining time = 4 hours - 0.5 hours = 3.5 hours.
   - For the remaining 3.5 hours, he drives at 80 mph.
     - Distance = 80 mph × 3.5 hours = 280 miles.

3. **Total Distance Traveled While Returning:**
   - Total distance = 0 miles (standstill) + 15 miles + 280 miles = 295 miles.

4. **Net Distance from Home:**
   - John initially drove 180 miles away from home.
   - He then traveled 295 miles back towards home.
   - Net distance from home = 180 miles - 295 miles = -115 miles.

Since distance cannot be negative, the absolute value represents the distance from home, which is 115 miles. However, since the question asks how far he is from home at the end of those 4 hours, we consider the net distance, which is 115 miles. But since he was initially 180 miles away and came back 295 miles, he is actually 115 miles closer to home than his starting point, but still 180 - 115 = 65 miles away from home.

Subtract this from the initial distance: 180 - 135 = 45 miles. So, John is 45 miles from home.

展开完整原始回答（非 600 字符摘录）
 To solve this problem, we need to calculate the total distance John travels in each segment of his journey and then determine his final position relative to his home.

1. **First Segment (Driving away from home):**
   - John drives for 3 hours at 60 mph.
   - Distance = Speed × Time = 60 mph × 3 hours = 180 miles.

2. **Second Segment (Returning home):**
   - John tries to get home in 4 hours.
   - First 2 hours: Standstill traffic, so distance covered = 0 miles.
   - Next 0.5 hours: Driving at 30 mph.
     - Distance = 30 mph × 0.5 hours = 15 miles.
   - Remaining time = 4 hours - 0.5 hours = 3.5 hours.
   - For the remaining 3.5 hours, he drives at 80 mph.
     - Distance = 80 mph × 3.5 hours = 280 miles.

3. **Total Distance Traveled While Returning:**
   - Total distance = 0 miles (standstill) + 15 miles + 280 miles = 295 miles.

4. **Net Distance from Home:**
   - John initially drove 180 miles away from home.
   - He then traveled 295 miles back towards home.
   - Net distance from home = 180 miles - 295 miles = -115 miles.

Since distance cannot be negative, the absolute value represents the distance from home, which is 115 miles. However, since the question asks how far he is from home at the end of those 4 hours, we consider the net distance, which is 115 miles. But since he was initially 180 miles away and came back 295 miles, he is actually 115 miles closer to home than his starting point, but still 180 - 115 = 65 miles away from home.

 To calculate Eliza's earnings for the week, we need to consider her regular pay for the first 40 hours and her overtime pay for the additional hours. 

First, we calculate her regular pay:
Regular pay = 40 hours * $10/hour = $400.

Next, we calculate her overtime pay. She worked 45 hours in total, so the overtime hours are 45 - 40 = 5 hours. Her overtime rate is 1.2 times her regular hourly rate, which is 1.2 * $10 = $12 per hour.

Overtime pay = 5 hours * $12/hour = $60.

Finally, we add her regular pay and overtime pay to find her total earnings:
Total earnings = Regular pay + Overtime pay = $400 + $60 = $460.

   - Since 16 glasses form 8 pairs, we multiply the cost of one pair by the number of pairs.

 Let's denote the number of sheep in Seattle as S, Charleston as C, and Toulouse as T. We know that S = 20. From the problem, we have the relationships:

1. Toulouse has twice as many sheep as Charleston: T = 2C.
2. Charleston has 4 times as many sheep as Seattle: C = 4S.

Substituting S = 20 into the second equation gives C = 4 * 20 = 80. Now, using C = 80 in the first equation gives T = 2 * 80 = 160.

To find the total number of sheep, we sum the sheep in all three cities: T + C + S = 160 + 80 + 20 = 260.

 To solve this problem, we need to calculate the total distance John travels in each segment of his journey and then determine his final position relative to his home.

1. **First Segment (Driving away from home):**
   - John drives for 3 hours at 60 mph.
   - Distance = Speed × Time = 60 mph × 3 hours = 180 miles.

2. **Second Segment (Returning home):**
   - John tries to get home in 4 hours.
   - First 2 hours: Standstill traffic, so distance covered = 0 miles.
   - Next 0.5 hours: Driving at 30 mph.
     - Distance = 30 mph × 0.5 hours = 15 miles.
   - Remaining time = 4 hours - 0.5 hours = 3.5 hours.
   - For the remaining 3.5 hours, he drives at 80 mph.
     - Distance = 80 mph × 3.5 hours = 280 miles.

3. **Total Distance Traveled While Returning:**
   - Total distance = 0 miles (standstill) + 15 miles + 280 miles = 295 miles.

4. **Net Distance from Home:**
   - John initially drove 180 miles away from home.
   - He then traveled 295 miles back towards home.
   - Net distance from home = 180 miles - 295 miles = -115 miles.

Since distance cannot be negative, the absolute value represents the distance from home, which is 115 miles. However, since the question asks how far he is from home at the end of those 4 hours, we consider the net distance, which is 115 miles. But since he was initially 180 miles away and came back 295 miles, he is actually 115 miles closer to home than his starting point, but still 180 - 115 = 65 miles away from home.

 To calculate Eliza's earnings for the week, we need to consider her regular pay for the first 40 hours and her overtime pay for the additional hours. 

First, we calculate her regular pay:
Regular pay = 40 hours * $10/hour = $400.

Next, we calculate her overtime pay. She worked 45 hours in total, so the overtime hours are 45 - 40 = 5 hours. Her overtime rate is 1.2 times her regular hourly rate, which is 1.2 * $10 = $12 per hour.

Overtime pay = 5 hours * $12/hour = $60.

Finally, we add her regular pay and overtime pay to find her total earnings:
Total earnings = Regular pay + Overtime pay = $400 + $60 = $460.
